ParlaMint-LV_2014-12-17-PT12-278-U55	Let us start a debate. Member Igor Pimenov applied for the debate.
ParlaMint-LV_2014-12-17-PT12-278-U56	Good afternoon, Madam President. Dear Members ( From the room: “Pearl the microphone!” “There’s nothing you can hear!” ), honourable Members! My first proposal is to be seen in conjunction with proposal 7, so I will also report on both. It is in the public interest to create a tax system that motivates entrepreneurs to spend their profits not on new cars or on entertainment, but on their own infrastructure and production. Personal spending can be reduced by increasing personal income tax on dividends, but it is a task of another law. We are encouraging this by applying dividends to the same personal income tax rate as that applied to wages, with the highest rate being set in proportion to the higher income. On the other hand, in the current proposal, I propose not to levy corporate tax on the proportion of profits invested in the company's assets, so as to motivate the business operator to invest in productive investments. If this rule is activated, it will encourage entrepreneurs not to hide profits, will not make them manipulate accounting in order to reduce the tax due. Does anyone know how big the real amount of business profits is in the country? We will never know that if we continue to tax profits on corporate income as we have done so far. This tax philosophy, which I set out, is being implemented in Estonia and is very effective. The rate of tax on the share of profits invested in commercial activities is zero per cent, whereas the personal income tax on dividends is 21 per cent. The critics of our proposal have often stressed that the tax system currently in force in the country is already well-positioned - with a number of discounts for companies, however, I believe it is excessively complex. Its aim is rather to create a tax corridor so as not to allow the company to find the bowls to get out of the grey economy, but I think that there should be an open space, not a corridor, where the businessman feels free to make the most of his profits. The proposal, which I have formulated and commented on, ensures greater freedom for our entrepreneurs, creates a motivation for entrepreneurs, which will lead to the businessman having an interest in investing in productive investments and thus contribute to the development of Latvia's economy. I call for support for proposals 1 and 7.

ParlaMint-LV_2014-12-17-PT12-278-U79	Let us start a debate. Name to Member Ivars Zarins.
ParlaMint-LV_2014-12-17-PT12-278-U80	Hello, colleagues! ... Can the microphone be adjusted louder? Yeah... Hello, colleagues! My proposal is about getting out... ( Internointing, Hall noise. Depp. V.Ageshin: “We don’t hear anything!” ; dep. I. Pastor: “Raise the voice!” )... Well, do I start yelling? ( From the drug: “Yes! ” ) My proposal relates to the fact that... I propose that we do not accept what is currently proposed in the current draft law - to pass on one separate cost to the transmission system operators involved in collecting, administering and meeting the settlement obligation with the public trader. Why does this not need to be done, and what are the risks if we leave such a proposal here? The regulator's methodology, the law on public services, as well as the electricity market law already state that the transmission system operator is entitled to cover all of its costs incurred by him when working effectively. This is the regulator's methodology. If we now separate one such item of expenditure and write it into the law - it is not really logical! - then we are in a very dangerous situation where, in such a case, the transmission system operator will be able to include any costs associated with meeting the settlement obligation with the public trader. What is it? This is about this famous component of compulsory procurement, which, as you know, is due to the support of local electricity producers who are paid a price of electricity that is higher than the market price. On the other hand, this price difference is covered by us as consumers, but this money is collected from consumers by these system operators. It's their duty... Thus, distribution system operators collect it from consumers connected to distribution system networks, while the transmission system operator in question is obliged to collect these payments from transmission system connected customers. And one of these consumers is “Liepājas metalurgs”. And now these amendments to the law, which are being proposed here... what I propose to remove from the field of this law legalises a situation where the transmission system operator can agree with a trader connected to his networks that he may not pay these mandatory procurement components... those additional components which he would have to pay. And this component he'll cover... will stick to the other consumer’s shoulders. So that is the proposal for this bill. Colleagues, I assume that, as a result, we will get very quickly to a European court for distortion of competition, because, in essence, this draft law provides that two traders, by agreement between the voluntari, can figure out that they are passing their costs on to the shoulders of other traders. That is the proposal that has been included in the law here. I call for such a proposal to be excluded. Thank you.

ParlaMint-LV_2014-12-17-PT12-278-U99	Let us start a debate. Member Gunārs Kūtris took part in the debate.
ParlaMint-LV_2014-12-17-PT12-278-U100	Hello, honourable Members! Although I am not the author of this proposal, I am addressing you in relation to this proposal. Dear colleagues, In this norm, without discussions and explanations, it is planned that Latvian citizens will continue to buy electricity from producers using renewable energy sources at a fixed price for another 20 years ahead... 20 years from the start of operation of the power plant, which means that of at least 13 power plants for a further seven years. Other points related to this transitional provision also specify that in reality the costs of these purchases will be borne by the final consumers of electricity - that is, the residents of Latvia. And according to this norm, and also in the proposal that seeks to improve this norm, it happens that the people of Latvia will have some burden - a burden imposed by the legislator only because now, when adopting the budget, it seems necessary to do so. Dear colleagues, Such a norm seriously offends a person, and it may be that citizens or entrepreneurs who do not explicitly perceive this regulation will be able to appeal to the Constitutional Court and challenge it, proving that this regulation restricts rights and that it has not been adopted by a law adopted in due course. The first paragraph of Article 87.1 of the Parliament's Rules of Procedure states exactly what draft laws can be merged into a budget package, namely those which define or amend a national budget. Five years ago, the Hungarian Constitutional Court declared several dozens of laws invalid because they were adopted improperly. In particular, these were laws that were not related to the next year's budget. In 2011, the Constitutional Court stressed in particular: “The Legislator has the right and at the same time the obligation to regulate only such issues which relate to the specific economic year and are closely related to the use of state funds in the Law on the State Budget and the package of the laws accompanying them.” Dear Members, I invite you to look at the author's own budgetary implications as an annotation of this draft law. It's all three years. It's written zero! For the population, for the final consumer's budget, this will, of course, be a matter of concern. There is no such violation in the state budget. Therefore, I call on Members to consider whether we will not undermine the authority of the Saeima by adopting a law that will be quite easy to challenge in the Constitutional Court and perhaps easily be repealed only because the law has been adopted inappropriately for the draft law. Thank you.
ParlaMint-LV_2014-12-17-PT12-278-U101	Thank you. The name is Mr. Vilnis Ķirsim.
ParlaMint-LV_2014-12-17-PT12-278-U102	President-in-Office, colleagues. I will talk about the substance of the proposals. In fact, because we have to pay this increased tariff for these wind stations, we can only thank, of course, the People's Party, which, if I am not mistaken, adopted such a law in 2002. But why not the 15 years proposed by Mr. Zarins, but 20 years? If it were in my power, I would be prepared to close this good tariff off at some point tomorrow, but we have to understand that if the country is committed, then, by way of derogation, we are simply putting ourselves at the risk of judicial proceedings, which can ultimately be paid even more expensive. And why 20 instead of 15? Because the wind station is simply a plant, as well as construction, and the common practice is that the construction investment, so to say, the useful life of use, or, as the bookkeeping says, depreciation, is 20 years. And that's why 20 years have been mentioned, not 15 years, because if there were less than 20 years, investors will be able to say in the court that they invested with the idea that they would be allowed to use the entire time limit to repay them, but if they knew it earlier, they would not have made that investment. So, I think, we can once again say thank you for this to the People's Party, but let us not bring the country into new judicial risks and will support, however, the 20 years proposed by the Cabinet of Ministers. Thank you.

ParlaMint-LV_2014-12-17-PT12-278-U207	Mr. Pimenov, one moment! Colleagues! ( knock with a hammer.) Reconciliate, please, quietly! The speaker can no longer be heard at the platform. Please continue!
ParlaMint-LV_2014-12-17-PT12-278-U208	Consumer taxes make the common tax system regressive. Rich residents of Latvia shift only part of their monthly income and only part of it that is diverted to consumption pays value added tax and excise duty. The rest of the country's population is forced to shift all their income towards consumption, thus from all (!) supplementing the State budget with its income. And it is the progressive nature of personal income tax that would be a way of mitigating the regressive nature of other taxes, because it directly affects the solvency of a person, but it is the principle of progressiveity that determines the highest tax burden for those who have more opportunities to earn. Meanwhile, the current rate of permanent income tax is taxed in proportion to its size, whereas dividends are subject to a significantly lower rate of income... income tax rate, which makes the entire system even regressive. Our proposal is based on a number of objectives. First. Similar rates of personal income tax should be applied to all types of income, both wage and capital gains (including dividends) and capital gains. Second. Instead of one existing rate, three income tax rates should be introduced for three different income ranges: 15 per cent if annual taxable income does not exceed 14 400 euro, 25 per cent if annual taxable income exceeds 14 400 euro but does not exceed 24 thousand euro and 30 per cent if annual taxable income exceeds 24 thousand euro. Objective three: The rates should be determined on the basis of the annual taxable income. The taxpayer of wages shall pay the tax on the monthly taxable income by calculating the annual income as a multiplication of the monthly income by 12. The progressive application of personal income tax will have consequences, as the English say, or overflow effects are very important. Firstly, the budget execution will increase at the expense of personal income tax due to the increase in the amount of tax due on taxable persons with significantly higher annual income. Secondly, the tax reduction will mitigate teachers' and healthcare workers' demands for wage increases. Thirdly, as the tax burden is shifted to high income, the labour tax burden will be reduced, which in turn reduces the costs for Latvian producers and increases their competitiveness. Fourthly, the reduction of the tax burden leads to an increase in the real income of workers and in the demand for the market, which will motivate entrepreneurs. Fifthly, an extremely high increase in demand will be local - directly local! - the goods market. This will contribute to the development of domestic production, motivate local producers to increase production volumes and contribute to an increase in the number of local producers. And, sixthly, the needs for social costs will decrease. We believe that it is progressivity that will help solve very many problems in the country of Latvia, it will stabilise it, it will stabilise society. I therefore call on the Saeima to support our proposals - Amendments 12 and 13. Thank you.

ParlaMint-LV_2014-12-17-PT12-278-U224	Colleagues, I would like to remind you that this year, in accordance with the Law on State Social Insurance, the right delegated to the Cabinet of Ministers to set the maximum or ceiling of the subject subject subject to social tax was restored. For income above this ceiling, taxpayers could not pay social tax. What does that mean? While the income is lower than these ceilings, the total amount of income tax and social tax due for the increase in income is higher, but if the income exceeds the maximum amount of social tax, the taxpayer has the possibility to no longer pay social tax, therefore the total amount of income tax and social tax payable is reduced significantly. And it happens that the recipients of high incomes pay a relatively lower tax than those with low wages. Thus, in the current legislation, labour tax on high income is regressive, which is socially unfair. In addition, this means that the State, but in particular the local authorities, receives relatively fewer taxes from the recipients of high revenues in their budgets. I propose the introduction of an additional personal income tax rate, which will be paid by the taxable person for income that goes beyond the currently established subject of compulsory state social insurance contributions. This additional rate will ensure that beneficiaries of high wages are subject to the same overall burden of labour tax above and below the social tax ceiling, and consequently regressivity will be eliminated. By the regulations of the Cabinet of Ministers of 17 December 2013, the State has determined the maximum amount of the object of social tax. This amount is EUR 46 400. The introduction of this social tax ceiling in 2015, next year, will have a negative overall impact on the budget, which will be equal to EUR 31.4 million, but if part of the wage that exceeds the social tax ceiling is subject to an additional personal income tax rate equal to 31.68 percent - this is the result of calculations - then there will be an identical positive impact on personal income tax revenues. So imagine, honourable members of the coalition, what that means for our budget! We would get more than EUR 31 million in revenue. Moreover, since December 2013, when the maximum amount of social tax was set, the number of persons covered by social security contributions to the social tax on salaries of over 46,400 euros has increased from 4 thousand to 6 thousand, and thus the positive effect will be even greater with the adoption of the proposal under consideration. I therefore ask you to support my proposal by voting in favour.

Information is needed to do so. Almost 25 years will have passed since the start of activities of municipalities in Latvia, but still this system is not arranged. We have been dealing with this issue of the compatibility of national data in different contexts from year to year with various responsible public officials and institutions, but this issue does not move from place to place. And since complexes have failed to resolve this issue, municipalities are trying to resolve this issue in a promising manner by agreeing with various state institutions. To a certain extent, cooperation between the State Revenue Service and municipalities has developed sufficiently collegial and good, but there are often bureaucratic obstacles that prevent the resolution of this issue to the end. Now, on the one hand, there is a signal from the State Revenue Service that we will provide data, but on the other hand, there is information that certain employees have been specifically told that they will not... until certain authorising rules are introduced in the law, however, the data will not be provided to municipalities. The municipalities will, of course, remain obliged to prove for what purpose these data are needed, and the municipalities are prepared to do so. ParlaMint-LV_2014-12-17-PT12-278-U285	Dear colleagues, The draft law proposed for evaluation contains perhaps many good and probably also necessary legal provisions. You can agree that the fight against taxpayers should be improved, yes, and that the person should be punished for offences. However, I think that if we say with certainty that Latvia is a state governed by law, then these funds... These measures must be implemented by legal means, namely by means compatible with the Constitution. If we look at Chapter 11 “Reimbursement of late tax payments of legal person” of the draft law, then we see that there are only three articles in it, and these articles show a clear contradiction with the three articles of the Satversme. Some will, of course, say that any law can be done wisely and well and, of course, also - apologies. - the dunes law can be applied correctly by a good performer, but I doubt very much whether the State Revenue Service always executes laws so precisely and there is no case when a person wins a dispute with the State Revenue Service. The rule of law must be such that the infringement cannot be committed... it would not be possible, first and foremost, because of the precise regulation of the law. Where are the violations of human rights laid down in the Constitution and the Convention for the Protection of Human Rights and Fundamental Freedoms in Chapter 11 of these contested norms? Article 91 of the Satversme - the principle of equality. This means that there can be no legal difference in treatment between persons under the same conditions. The duty to reimburse the tax debt is limited to the members of the board of a capital company. Why does it not apply to personal property vehicles and represent eligible members? Why does this not apply to the decision-making bodies of the legal person, which may also have the right to accept such issues that are in agreement with the Governing Board? What about the shareholder of SIA, who can dispose of certain assets? What about the procurators of such a society? If someone maybe doesn't know the Commercial Law, then I just... to understand the responsibility of the party or the responsibility of the party... A small comparison. If someone who isn't... a member of the governing board of a party who is on the governing board of a party has taken one decision, but the general meeting or the board takes that decision within the competence of the governing board, but another decision which makes a binding decision, the member of the governing board shall nevertheless be responsible for the decision taken by the council or the general meeting. This is an analogy to this situation. They're going to be held accountable, they're not. The second article. Article 105 of the Satversme - right to property. If a person is required to reimburse from his property another person, the legal person, the liability arising, namely the tax debt, it is a restriction on the right to property. In order to assess whether the restriction of human rights is acceptable, the legitimate objectives have been established in Article 116 of the Satversme. And if we look at what the legitimate aim is, in an annotation of this bill, then... The efficiency of tax collection and administration has been increased. That is, if the public administration, the public authority, fails to meet their responsibilities, then the legitimate aim... it can be moved to the shoulders of another person, to the shoulders of an individual. And here, the country has set its legitimate goal through the norm. And finally. Constitutional Law 40. ... I am sorry, Article 92, which contains the principle of presumption of innocence. If someone says that this subject is not criminal law, I will agree in part, but remember that the European Court of Human Rights has said that the penalty procedure and the fine or the amount of money imposed are of a criminal nature and that the principle of presumption of innocence is therefore applied here. And here, in our draft law under discussion, there is a requirement for a person to prove his or her integrity. Mr Shadurski said that the Commission has addressed this problem. No, it’s just about people saying: “I didn’t have those documents available... “

ParlaMint-LV_2014-12-17-PT12-278-U361	Article 51 of the Law on Higher Education “Determining the Number of Study Places” states: “The number of study places to be financed from state budget in higher education is determined by the Minister of Education and Science on a proposal from the Higher Education Council. The number of study places in universities founded by other legal and natural persons shall be determined by the founders of these universities.’ Article 52 “Studies fee and scholarships”: “State determines the number of study places in universities and colleges financed from state budget funds. The procedure by which universities and colleges are financed from the state budget shall be determined by the Cabinet of Ministers. Admission to state-funded study places shall be subject to competition.’ These are the articles of the law. And then. Higher education requires a clear public order based on research on the professions necessary for development of Latvia. This would allow a useful use of state budget resources, as well as clear signals to students about their future prospects. This would finally eliminate duplication of programmes in different universities, which reduces quality, because the resources for reciprocal competition for paid students are wasted, although it could be the opposite! - leads to a decrease in the quality of higher education. In most areas, as evidenced by data from the international evaluation of programmes, this system of budget allocation should be reviewed, because Latvia is not so rich that it could afford to finance higher education as it has been up to now. There is a huge lack of strategic planning system in the country as a whole, as each ministry plans within its own responsibility, but as a result no one is able or willing to see the system's interaction and long-term development of the country as a whole. The adoption of the budget is not the adoption of figures, but the adoption of policies. We must finally make a distinction in order to clean up universities and not waste money, but provide public universities with adequate funding. Thank you.

ParlaMint-LV_2014-12-17-PT12-278-U500	Dear colleagues, By submitting amendments to the draft law on the medium-term budgetary framework for 2015, 2016 and 2017, which provides for the restoration of the state's membership in financial provision of the housing allowances, I am thinking of those Latvian citizens whose rights have been violated since the beginning of the 1990s, when the denationalisation of housing property was implemented in the country. More than twenty years have passed, but there is still a profit-making business in Latvia, which is known by the nation as a pie with and without meat trade, which I am already talking about from this tribunal last time. Both the State Police, the Ombudsman's Office and other responsible institutions register complaints of residents of denationalised houses of desperation about the coercion of household owners. People are sometimes literally thrown out on the street without property and documents, years of life without heating, without water, without sewerage and without permanent danger, and are faced with total helplessness to ensure respect for human rights - the right to housing and the integrity of housing - guaranteed by the Constitution. When we voted in favour of the amendments I tabled to the medium-term budgetary framework for the next three years, which could provide for an annual allocation of EUR 4 million of EUR 760 000 306, allowing the Ministry of Economic Affairs to restore the compensation of the inhabitants of denationalised houses, we will be able to confirm the legislative power's readiness and ability to control the executive powers, because yesterday, at the meeting of the Budget and Financial (Tax) Commission, I listened to Vjačeslav Dombrovsky's sad story that the Ministry of Economics is unable to steer this issue as a matter of priority. And so for six years now, the government has been ignoring the pain of the denationalised house dwellers, not seeing and not hearing people whose human rights are sometimes violated so grossly that when they go into it, the feeling that you are living in a civilised European country is lost. When a 74-year-old weeps from the happiness that he finally stays at night not in a shelter, but is placed in a social care centre, when state police officers are forced to spend long evenings and even night hours at the back door of the apartment, we can no longer be blind and deaf. We have to act! Yesterday's vote by the Commission for Human Rights and Public Affairs on amendments to the Civil Procedure Law, which provides for the introduction of a legal mechanism for the protection of tenants' rights by temporary civil procedure, took a very important step in this direction, thus acknowledging the need to protect the human rights of citizens of denationalised houses. Today it is up to us to take our step, a courageous step, which will, of course, be contrary to the position of the government, but it will be in full compliance with the interests of society and with the responsibilities of the legislators. Once again, I call on you to support my amendments to the draft law on the medium-term budgetary framework for 2015, 2016 and 2017. Thank you.

As a result, a number of investment projects are ‘frozen’. We are worried, for example, about the following. In the beginning of 2014, the Association of Latvian Enterprises, which includes joint stock company “Zasulauks”, Daugavpils Locomotive Repair Plant and Riga Vagon Building Plant, launched the implementation of the project “Modernisation of the Rolling Stock of Diesel Trains” co-financed by the Cohesion Fund. Modernization will improve the level of passenger comfort, safety, environmental protection and energy economy and, above all, create new jobs and improve the working conditions of existing servicing staff. And already there are four train compositions that have already been dismantled, and the preparation of these train compositions for processing takes place. The above-mentioned wagons are located in the Riga wagon construction plant, while the installation of engines and control systems is intended for the Daugavpils Locomotive Repair Plant. However, at a time when the project is under threat, when the company lacks funds, co-financing for the implementation of this project is necessary, because without this project, not only modernisation of trains will be jeopardised, but it will also not ensure that the objectives of the European Union Cohesion Fund project are achieved and that the funds allocated by the Fund are recovered. So, too, the funds that have been spent so far will go to the wind. Moreover, without this project, there will be serious financial problems... First of all, the Daugavpils Locomotive Repair Plant and the Riga Vagon construction plant, where the consequences of Russian economic sanctions have already been felt, have rapidly decreased turnover and the amount of work done. Honorable colleagues, currently there are at least 1000-1200 jobs in Daugavpils, the company is considering both optimisation of operations and reduction of states. We allow it to be... It may be that the scenario of Liepaja Metalurgs would be repeated in Daugavpils. Thus, in order to ensure the successful implementation of this project and the modernisation of the rolling stock of diesel trains, a State funding of EUR 8.98 million is needed. I call for support for this proposal - to provide national co-financing for this project. Thank you.

ParlaMint-LV_2014-12-17-PT12-278-U738	Dear colleagues, I will talk about the proposals of the Group's Heart for Latvia - Amendments 96, 97 and 98. The process of discussion and adoption of the State Budget and related draft laws in 2015 is an additional argument in favour of the proposal of the Group “From the Heart to Latvia” to establish, on the basis of the Centre for the Coordination of Reconvenients, an institution independent of the executive, which, gradually developing its capacity, would form a solid research base for the study of the national strategic issues and political decision-making. The need for such an institution is evident. In the last three months, both the Ministry of Economics and the Bank of Latvia have had to make substantial adjustments to their forecasts. This shows that there is something wrong in this area. Moreover, at a time when other countries of the European Union are becoming increasingly concerned about development issues, the budget drawn up by our government is still, in terms of development, largely only addressing health, teachers' salaries and infrastructure problems and thirsty gaps. It's just a rumoring policy! Last year, we launched a policy of backwardness and, unfortunately, it will continue this year as well. It's a dangerous way! The National Strategic Analysis and Planning Office could be a serious turning point to think about the future. The budgetary process shows a failure of the executive to assess the long-term impact of decisions. Or else we would often hear warnings from professionals, non-governmental organisations and the Legal Bureau of the Saeima about possible non-compliance of legal norms with the Satversme, about additional administrative burden, about failure to take into account the application of legal norms in practice? While driving new legislative initiatives, the whole discussion is limited to the vote "in favour" or "unsupported" but there is no assessment and discussion of how the new rules will affect the development of individual sectors and of the economy as a whole, both in the short and long term. The government, having understood it, is trying to break down the small amount that will remain over the next year after the external debt obligations have been paid out. But let us be realistic! It will not be possible to fill these gaps in the budget next year or in the next two years. Can we afford two years of thinking only about today’s needs? Let me have some doubts. The budgetary process itself shows that the country does not have a plan for real economic growth. There is not even a vision of priority sectors, the discussion is not about EUR 7.5 billion, and the Saeima is not giving executive powers the tasks that need to be solved and carried out in health, education, science, but listens to the box as the government allows the allocation of one million euro to 150 separate projects proposed by the co-legislators. There is no guarantee that the inadequate time to develop, evaluate and adopt quality decisions will not lead to wrong decisions, the consequences of which we will have to deal with in the future. The system of strategic planning and analysis established in the country is weak, despite the fact that both ministries, Cabinet of Ministers and Saeima and the Presidential Chancellery have resources for strategic planning. We believe that it is high time to improve the capacity of national policy analysis by attracting highly qualified experts and scientists. Once again, it is necessary to establish the State Strategic Analysis and Planning Office, whose main task would be to prepare proposals for long-term development of the country and economic growth, and to analyse the impact of decisions taken by the Cabinet of Ministers on our society, its welfare and economic growth. It's the last time to start watching the future! In the Committee on Budgets and Finances (taxes), when we examined our proposal, we were accused of the proposal being premature - 24 years after the restoration of independence and 10 years after its inclusion in the European Union, it is still too early to think about the future. Well, let's be honest about it: yes, we won't solve the issues of teachers' salaries, education quality and scientific development! But then, in a few years' time, do not be surprised that we do not have the human resources to deliver economic growth. The office of the future must be and become the State's mind and real advisors and executive powers. Therefore, please act wisely and support our proposals 96, 97 and 98. Thank you.

ParlaMint-LV_2014-12-17-PT12-278-U777	€20 million for increased pay for healthcare workers. Before giving an explanation, I would like to make a foreword. I would like to remind you that, in this Chamber, our colleagues who were Members of the 11th Saeima took an active part in a series of discussions on what funding is needed to provide healthcare that is worthy of the people of Latvia. Despite pre-election promises, the country’s total health budget has however increased to 3 percent of its gross domestic product. This is unforgivable so that the system can work properly and that it can be demonstrated in real terms that the health sector is being prioritised by both words and programmes of some parties. In the meantime, recent data show that government expenditure on health care as a percentage of gross domestic product is as follows: in the European Union on average 7.3 and in the new Member States on average 5.2. We're at 3 percent. In Latvia health care due to its cost cannot cost 14 percent of the population, whereas in Estonia and Lithuania it is about 1 percent. Latvia has the most low-income population in comparison to other EU Member States, i.e. 30 percent who do not receive timely medical treatment. In the European Union this figure is on average 10 percent, in Lithuania it is 4 percent. Therefore, in Latvia (compared to the average mortality rate in the new European Member States in the age group up to 64), around 1400 people die from the main causes of death within a year. The Ministry of Health has estimated that EUR 106 million is needed to enable the health system to work properly. Of these, EUR 30 million is needed to raise staff remuneration, which in turn would allow to attract new and retain (i.e.) "existing employees in health sector workplaces and in Latvia". The Ministry's calculations have been discussed on several occasions in different audiences and no one has ever questioned the accuracy of the calculations. In consultations with industry representatives - with the Latvian Health and Social Care Workers' Union, with the Latvian Medical and Care Workers' Union - the human resources problem is a priority and can be accepted. After all, there is a real danger of losing workers! What will the renovated buildings and new equipment give us if there is no one to work with? Remember, to buy good equipment, you can earn money and still buy it, but to prepare a certified nurse, she needs to learn five years, doctor - 12 years. A specialist who goes to Western Europe to work and receive a salary three times higher than in Latvia will not return here soon. That's what I wanted to say. With the agreement of the industry, we recommend an increase of EUR 20 million in the budget for health care, with the aim of using these funds to increase the remuneration calculated by the Ministry of Health. I would remind you: the estimated figure is 30 million, allocated 8.2, but it needs at least 20 million extra. Vote for! ( From a faction, Unity: “No! ” ) Council Regulation (EC) No 1257/96 of 20 June 1996 concerning humanitarian aid (OJ L 163, 2.7.1996, p. 1).

ParlaMint-LV_2014-12-17-PT12-278-U1054	Dear colleagues, The fraction “From the heart to Latvia” invites to delete the words and the number “without applying the provisions of Section 30 of this Law”. We are all well aware that the words “effective cash flow” and “the rational use of financial resources” actually mean the desire of the manager of financial resources, in this case the Cabinet of Ministers, to obtain the delegation of the Saeima freely to dispose of resources, redistributing them at its own discretion. That is what the proposed draft law actually contains. We believe that the Saeima has already given the government free space in the budgeting process, in fact giving up its powers enshrined in the Constitution. Article 30 empowers the Minister of Finance to re-allocate appropriations between programmes and sub-programmes, as well as specifying the cases in which reallocation of appropriations is not permitted. The delegation provided for in Article 31(4) shall, however, give the Cabinet of Ministers an authorisation to carry out any reallocation. What happens if the government's capacity is too wide is seen at the moment, at the end of this year, when the government transfers the money to spend only the state budget. There are no questions and responsibility for poor quality budgetary planning, for new policy initiatives that prove to be not to be implemented at the end of the year. We do not have information on what planned objectives will not be met and how this will affect the necessary resources of the state budget. Why does the Ministry of Finance not act responsibly and do not propose to build the reserve recommended by the Fiscal Discipline Council on the basis of savings? We call on Members of the Saeima not to give up our executive powers as legislator. If Members of the Saeima have approved specific new policy initiatives or expenditures for specific purposes, such as ensuring the Latvian Presidency, then it is the law that the government has to implement, but the Saeima has to control that it will be fulfilled. If funds for any purpose are required to a lesser extent than those provided by the Saeima, the legislator must state that the government will not be able to amend them according to its own mind, but that they will have to be used to build a financial reserve. And then this reserve can only be divided by the Saeima itself. This will be particularly important next year, as the Ministry of Finance forecasts for 2016, its fiscal space is far from optimistic. That is why I am asking Members: do not connect your own hands and support our group's proposal. Thank you.

We propose to supplement the draft law with Article 43, which provides for the transfer of the funds received to two very important components of the health care system - rehabilitation and health promotion, which did not receive support for implementation of new policies of the Ministry of Health in the requested financial section of the draft state budget. Rehabilitation and the promotion of health are crucial for us to reduce the financial need for treatment which is growing regularly from one year to the next. We are no longer talking about the whole set of health services when it comes to funding for health care, but we are talking practically about treatment. Why are these two chapters important - rehabilitation and health promotion? Some figures and facts. First of all, rehabilitation is extremely necessary to reduce some very negative trend - the rate of first-time disability award and the amount of new arrivals of people with disabilities in our country every year. Currently there are 164 578 disabled people in Latvia, but last year the first disability was awarded to 17 600 people, which was the highest in the last eight years. Although these eight-year statistics are also available, this figure is not less than 10 thousand. It's bigger! And these are huge numbers, which again means that people's quality of life is, first of all, poor, low. Secondly, it is expenditure in the social budget. In order to reduce and prevent these things, we need to invest more in rehabilitation, which could help many people to stay in the labour market, stay in normal quality of life and not enter the status of disabled people. Further on health promotion. Just a couple of arguments. Latvia is in third place in Europe in terms of the number of suicides. About 400-500 people a year leave their lives voluntarily. This situation is affected by insufficient assistance for them in the area of mental care, addiction and various other problems that can be addressed by measures to promote health. In Latvia, 48 percent of men and 28 percent of women die under the age of 65.

It must be monitored, created and then requested responsibility from the relevant authorities. This is the task of the Saeima! Over the years, public spaces have been reprehensible about the low quality of education and science. Studies are being carried out to find out how low this quality is, but very little is being done to improve quality. Time is extended by recording tasks in regulatory enactments to the Cabinet of Ministers, but at the same time extending and extending the deadline for performing these tasks and not even requesting clarification of the reasons for the failure of the tasks. There is talk of improving the quality of science, closing weak study programmes and preventing the duplication of state-funded programmes (by subtracting each one but not investing enough in anything) and monitoring the effective use of these funds to achieve their objectives. It's about putting the school network in order. Quite regularly, without additional funding, the Ministry of Education and Science finds it in its own internal resources, and no one complains that someone else would have been taken away. This clearly indicates that the education and science system does not clearly define the results to be achieved and the quality management system to achieve these objectives. We believe that it is important to take important steps to strengthen the legislative power: by approving the state budget, to define the tasks to be carried out by the Cabinet for the improvement of the education system. And they are not individual measures, but a set of measures with a specific timetable, so that you can also claim responsibility from the Cabinet of Ministers and the Ministry of Education and Science. We propose to develop both a calculation system for allocating funding from the scientific base to the scientific institutions, as well as a funding procedure that ensures that specialists are trained in universities according to the demand of the labour market. It is equally important to ensure the management of the entire education process by applying the principles of the quality management system of education and evaluating all educational processes, starting with the network of establishment of schools, the content of education, the salaries of educators, as well as addressing the issue of adult non-formal education and improvement of professional competence. I invite you to support these proposals, as it will demonstrate the interest of the Saeima or the legislator in improving the operation of the education system in the near future, as well as provide an opportunity to monitor the performance of the tasks assigned to the education sector. Since education is one of the highest priorities of the country and its quality depends not only on the quality of life and growth of our future generations, we propose that the legislator take responsibility for the decisions made and monitor their implementation. I call at last for a systemic approach to the education and science system and economic growth. Thank you.

ParlaMint-LV_2014-12-17-PT12-278-U1085	If nothing extraordinary happens, I'll talk last night. (Applause) So, 3 million or 12 million? Investing in education contributes to economic growth. According to the Global Monitoring Report Education for All, 1 dollar invested in education contributes to the economy – 10 to 15 dollars. The UNESCO study also shows that raising teachers' pay improves the quality of education and student performance. From 1995 to 2005 in 39 countries the salary of teachers increased by 15 percent, and this has improved the performance of pupils by 6 to 8 percent. These studies do not refer to an increase in teachers' pay for part-euro per month, but rather to a significant and sensational increase in pay. The country should, at last, not give up on education every year, but make effective, sustainable, smart investment in education and meaningful, smart reforms in the sector. In order for the reform of the teachers' salary model, currently prepared by the Ministry of Education and Science, to have the desired impact on the quality of education and, in the long term, on economic growth, the increase in teachers' wages must be significant. This would enable the most capable people to be attracted to the teaching work, as well as create considerable competition as a basis for an increase in the quality of the teaching work. And is that not the aim of raising wages - to increase the quality of education in order to ensure economic growth, economic development and prosperity? In addition, this money is set aside from the emergency funds, which means that if the reform of the teacher's remuneration model is not completed in September, Members will have the opportunity to decide again whether to use this money or leave it to a reserve fund. I call, however, for the 12 million votes to be made. Thank you.
